# EARF approved pictures: the one list every asset draws from

Started 9 Nov 2026, 21:10 UTC, at the owner's direction ("we need to sequence the work, so the marketing and brand assets use the
right product renders"). Kept by the coordinator. A picture goes on this list only after it passes the blind gate (majority SHOW)
and the studio honesty rules (nothing hidden from the camera standing in frame; one sun, the sky's own; every object casts shadows).
The site, the Brand Book, social, the deck and press use ONLY pictures on this list. When a picture is replaced, the old line moves to
"Pulled" with the reason, and every asset that used it is refreshed in one batch.

## The order of work
1. LOCK EACH PRODUCT'S DESIGN AND MATERIALS. Tropos: locked (base A, no light; the polished horn; one dye per tint from
   tints-trucast.json), except Haze's horn pour (walkthrough 132). Lamps: DESIGN LOCKED 9 Nov 21:12 (the owner: Core B, "assuming we're still
   using the same materials we would use for the speakers and mantle": the outer ball in the speaker's TruCast tints, one dye per tint;
   a 3 mm white opal inner ball, the same opal as Hemisphere's diffuser); the lamps lane to confirm the CAD, BOM and stock in writing.
   The lamp kit's Ember dye was a stale hand value until 9 Nov, now the speaker's 2423. Mantle: Strata Float v3 AS SIGNED by the owner 1 Nov ~12:10 ("Mantle: A"; r46-owner-notes 5b63e05b; built 2fddab95..71d3163e).
   Everything after it is an unsigned proposal: the 11-03 edge-lit edit (8a20b821), v4 the lit deck, v5 option C/C3. Its light is open: v3's
   COB loop under the base, whose underglow the owner called wrong on 3 Nov.
   REOPENED 10 Nov 00:28 by the owner. Core: the inner opal ball concentric with the outer ("the outer core should be the same depth
   around the whole inner core like in the original images"). Hemisphere: "it should work like core cut in half": the light through the
   dome takes the shell's tint and the downward light is the bulb's color, so a source that lights the liner replaces the as-drawn
   downlight. Tropos: the owner asked whether the horn's posts (bronze, bare satin, in the parts list; painted brass in the pictures, the model having no bronze colour) should be black like the lamps' parts; nothing
   changes until he answers. Every lamp picture waits for the redrawn designs.
   ANSWERED 10 Nov ~02:05 (the page "Lamps and Rods", artifact RMwPzKNYYCmQtCAXuL3Vn1 v1; each answer an investigator and two checkers):
   Core concentric: the grommet raised (12.0 mm at Ø120), the core bonded on a spigot, the bore bright, lit by the LED only; the SIZE
   is open (Ø120 20 mm band, ~11 mm of black tube under the core in the hero view; Ø130 ~15; Ø140 ~10, a 4 mm gap): proxies first, then
   the CAD. Hemisphere option 3: an up-facing LED ring at the mouth's centre under a stock side-emitting lens (its IES file; the rim
   band at least 2/3 of the middle), a white crown, the opal to z 0; the TABLE LIGHT is open (575 lm ~120 lux; ~1,000 lm ~200 lux;
   or a puck below the rim). Rods: asked "Black or brass?" (my pick black). Owned to the owner: my 21:06 "October used the two-layer
   build" (wrong: one ball, a faked glow), my 22:34 ring recommendation ("glows evenly"; it glowed faint, pale teal), and my 22:38
   "Core B carries exactly the drawn LED's light" (an assumption; as drawn about half). Lamps lane briefed ~02:05.
   OWNER DECISIONS 10 Nov 02:15-02:45 (in chat): RODS BLACK ("Black rods I think"; acrylic rods ruled out as too flexible and brittle):
   the lamps' satin black #18181A on the posts and sockets, black-anodised 6061 in the parts list (speaker 9ac1ce88, 156c69ad, the asset
   810bdeed; the rod sizing to re-check for aluminium); every Tropos studio picture re-rendered in one batch on the speaker's container
   (fronts tallr7, horn-backs and turns p2b). SMOKE = neutral gray, 50 % through 20 mm, black lining (c4 ab9df168 g50: bark 34.7/m,
   horn 95/m); stock code or custom batch to be settled with chips; Smoke joins the same batch. CORE BAND 10 mm = a Ø140 core, concentric
   (the former to confirm the 4 mm gap; fallback Ø130, 15 mm). THE GLOBE (logo): not the speakers' acrylic; the speakers' colours at full
   strength ("The pale colors are not right"): A's look with dust #F78B34, acid #E4DE3C, ice #3C91EB (each cabinet dye taken deeper),
   the rest as the cabinets; the brand lane renders it once (A2). Still open: Hemisphere's table light, the Mantle's lighter red and
   posts, the chips. TOKEN ECONOMY from 02:10 (the owner near his weekly limit): no critic panels or research workflows; the coordinator
   checks each picture once; a lane renders only the owner's pick, once; habitats and the Book wait; everything he needs goes in chat.
   OWNER DECISIONS 10 Nov ~02:50 (in chat): HEMISPHERE = option 3 with the bigger ring, about 1,000 lm on the same driver, about 200 lux
   under the lamp ("your pick I guess"); a heat test needed. MANTLE = stock Ember, SATIN edge as signed ("I think the satin edge will
   help with light dispersion"), posts BLACK; the custom pour dropped; its light still open. SAMPLES: yes (chips and thick off-cuts,
   ~$150-200; the list goes to him for the purchase). Lamps and Mantle lanes briefed: CAD/BOM plus five lamp proofs and one Mantle final.
   OWNER 10 Nov ~03:00-03:15 (in chat): THE GLOBE A2 REJECTED ("you need to assess each color against the richer speaker colors.
   That blue is still not as strong or deep, nor are any of the colors"): measured on the tallr6 fronts, the globe AS RENDERED must
   reach ember #B50404, dust #D35D17, acid #C5BC1E, poison #027614, ice #1169C1, haze #500CB4 (the OKLab midpoint of each horn and its
   cabinet's richest area); the brand lane renders A3 with a measure-and-correct loop. THE BOOK SHOWS THE BAR (Planet EARF, Mother EARF,
   EARFcoin as where EARF is going). SMOKE IS INCLUDED: eight colors in the Book and on the site once its pictures land. THE MANTLE
   final (r48-mantle-design cc3c51ba; stock Ember, satin edges, black-oxide posts) NOT YET APPROVED: "Maybe the mantle is too dark.
   It's hard to tell without the backlit example and other colors"; the Mantle lane renders all eight by day and three lit frames
   (real LEDs only), the bearing's brass in black as a labeled variant. The Book audit and plan: coordinator/book-audit-2026-11-10/.
   THE GLOBE A3 APPROVED by the owner 10 Nov ~03:27 ("Globe is good"): r47-brand 3a1342cc, A3-translucent-matched-colors.png and its
   .hues.json (measured against the targets: ember, dust, acid, poison within 0.02 OKLab, ice 0.03, haze 0.07 at the gamut edge). The
   logo's colours are now the speakers': the flat marks and chips take ember #B50404, dust #D35D17, acid #C5BC1E, poison #027614,
   ice #1169C1, haze #500CB4. The brand lane renders the final mark set and the spin from A3 (honest lights); I check one sheet; then
   the Book's single rebuild on my go, when the black-rods and Smoke pictures land.
   TAGLINE, the owner 10 Nov ~03:35: "Globe is good" (his own phrase, "a play on 'god is good'"). The main line stays "A world of
   sound, in color."; "Globe is good." is the short sign-off under the globe (stickers, the card, social posts), and the Book shows

   THE OWNER ON THE SITE PREVIEW (dpl_H1erTkHj, r49-site-rc 6e7250d0), 10 Nov ~09:25: "The site needs more work. The initial loading
   globe has a white background or stroke that peeks through behind it now. The decibel values and such don't make sense on a home
   page that contains other products. We're not leading with really captivating imagery anymore. The initial photos belong on the
   speaker detail pages. We need strong hero imagery for the home page, like the speakers placed in a blended biome that represents
   the troposphere. That's just one idea. Open to others. Might also be cool to lead with an image that shows all of the products
   together in a room with different color combos. Hovering a product reveals what it is and links to it. Point being, we can do
   better. Needs to be more aspirational." So: the site lane fixes the globe's ground, takes the figures and the row of fronts off
   the home page, leads with the Ice habitat for now and builds a hotspot hero; the biomes lane proposes (in words first) a
   troposphere landscape hero of the eight; the room hero of all four products waits for the Mantle and lamp decisions.
   HERO: the biomes lane proposed three; the coordinator picked (b), the eight on a ridge above a sea of cloud at sunrise, the low sun
   through each cabinet laying its colour on the rock (one scene, one sun; a low-res proxy first, then the owner).
   THE OWNER'S READ OF BOOK v26, 10 Nov ~14:45, verbatim: "Under identity, the globe rendered on dark doesn't work anymore. We need to
   make the same adjustments we did last time. I don't think we need the globe drawn like it is. We can use the monochromatic globe
   and color in each tile to match the rendered globe instead as the multi-color option. The turn animation is flashing the reflection
   abruptly again, making the animation look less smooth. Don't use the term cheeky replace it with something similar. Don't use
   'ember to smoke,' say the full spectrum or all wavelengths or something like that. I don't get the clear space section. It's not
   aligned with the other section headers. This might be too much text for someone to get through. I like the social post section.
   More of those might be good. It looks like you removed some of the images for marketing materials, which I liked. Also the bar and
   earfcoin seem important to include." RULING THAT FOLLOWS: THE MARK IS AN ILLUSTRATION, NOT A PRODUCT PICTURE; the product-picture
   light rules (no dimmed reflections, no diffuse-only fill, no emitting acrylic) do NOT bind the globe, its lockups or its turn. The
   three adjustments the coordinator had removed at 01:03 are restored on the mark by the owner's direction. Product pictures keep
   every rule. Book v27 briefed to the brand lane: the dark globe and the turn as before, the flat mark as coloured tiles, "Wry, but
   reserved.", "Eight colors, the full spectrum.", half the text, nine social posts, the marketing mockups back, a chapter for the
   bar and EARFcoin, the two black-rods habitats.

   attractive product if done right though. See similar products like brian eno's record player, there's a marantz one, etc." The
   white base was the coordinator's proposal, not a decision. Clear tinted acrylic cannot glow from within; the Mantle lane renders a
   "frosted" proposal (plinth and base in one frosted tint; by day unlit, and lit from inside), Ember and Ice.
   LAMP LIGHT PER TINT: "For the lights, I think some need warm and some need amber. Ice loses its blue with amber, but from below
   I like ember with amber." So the LED colour is set per tint, not offered twice on every tint: Ember amber and Ice warm are his;
   the coordinator's proposed split for the rest (to confirm): Dust and Acid amber; Poison, Haze, Fog and Smoke warm. The lamps lane
   gets this with his three open lamp answers (Core's brightness, Core's base, Hemisphere's go), in one brief.

   RULE (third time: plinth, satin horn, old Mantle): a scene may place a product only from that product's approved asset

   THE BAR (owner, 11 Nov 15:41 UTC): "Everything has to look classy and refined and not cheap, even though it's acrylic."
   Standing rule for every part and every picture. Finish and detail only: a signed shape or size does not change without
   the owner; lanes list those instead.
   1. No gloss black plastic. Black parts are satin (bead-blasted, anodized, black oxide), not dead matte: form must read.
   2. One hardware finish across the line, satin black. No chrome, polished steel, bare aluminum, brass or zinc. Bright only
      where a bought or working part cannot be black, and listed.
   3. Nothing that looks like a leftover of making: clips, tabs, snap fits, exposed screw heads, part lines, glue lines,
      stickers, loose wire, LED dots, boards. Hidden, or a deliberate detail in the one finish.
   4. Acrylic is thick, edges polished with a small even chamfer or radius. No thin sheet, no raw sharp edge.
   5. Shapes read as turned or machined, not molded. No buckets, cups or lumps.
   6. Cord, cap and ceiling parts are part of the product: cloth-covered cord, cap and canopy in the one finish.
   7. Pictures: no flat even light, gray void, washed color or CG-looking stand-in furniture. Deep color held in the acrylic,
      long soft highlights along its edges, darker and richer grounds, real scanned materials. The set looks as expensive as
      the product. Honesty rules unchanged.

   - Fit: a small change, not a drop-in. Plate 360 x 120 x 55 (FA122: 315), cutout 336 x 96 (291); the compartment

     FA122, NOT +5 (maximum about 117 -> 119.6 dB at 1 m at 100 Hz; 116 -> 118 at 63 Hz). The 9 Nov note's "+5.2 dB,
     75 -> 250 W" overstated it. CAUTION: Hypex's minimum bridged load is 4 ohm and it recommends 8 or more; the
     15PR400-8's minimum is 5.55-5.9 ohm. Ask Hypex.
   - Horn driver on the NC100HF: unchanged in practice (about 22 V, 31 W; noise 40 uV maximum). Heat: idle about 11 W
     against 7.5 W; within Hypex's guidance; the 62368 pre-check still needed. Cost: +$158.40 a pair plus about $2.
   - If it goes ahead: `cloud.py` and `product.py` (the part, amp_z, the box height, the cutout, the inserts, the

   THE CURSOR FIX IS NOT LIVE (r49 942f0727c9, r47 f48140250f): in Chromium it does what was asked (my test
   on its preview at 1440 x 900: hidden at load, shown after a real move, hidden after leaving, shown on
   return, no errors), but it drops every pointermove whose movementX and movementY are zero, and WebKit
   has reported 0 there for a mouse on macOS (bug 220194, said fixed in Safari 15.5) and undefined on iOS
   (bug 248119, fixed in Safari 17); on such a browser the custom cursor would never appear (confirmed on
   the preview with two dispatched moves that carry no movement values). Sent back 11:10 UTC: show on the
   first move whose position differs from the remembered one, with a five-point test.
   17 Nov 11:24 UTC, THE CURSOR FIX IS LIVE (main a2d66f6e11 = r49-site-rc a2d66f6e11, r47-site-next 1ec327281c; pushed
   11:20 UTC by a plain fast-forward from e4c4e0265d; changes: the inline script of the 21 pages, the same ten
   lines in each, and the generator; vercel.json unchanged; the public site served it from 11:22 UTC).
   What it does: on a mouse device the dot and ring stay hidden until the pointer has truly moved (the first
   pointermove only records its position; a later one at another position shows the cursor with the ring on
   the pointer), they hide when the mouse leaves the window, and the position is forgotten then. It no
   longer reads movementX or movementY. Before, Chromium's own move at load, at the pointer's resting
   place, turned the cursor on; with no mouse position known that was the page's top-left corner.
   MY TESTS (Chromium, 1440 x 900), on the preview dpl_v5aT91EqWmD7c4ANBixGAPvvfHuL (/, /colors/ember,
   /tropos, /#hemisphere-ember) and again on www.earf.world (/, /#mantle-smoke): hidden at load (ring off
   screen, opacity 0; before it sat at -17, -17 with opacity 1); after the mouse has left: one dispatched
   move without movement values stays hidden, the same position again stays hidden, a different position
   shows it with the ring there; a real move shows it; leaving hides it; returning shows it; a touch
   pointermove does not move it; a click on a gallery thumbnail and on a home link leave it on; no page
   errors; phones have no cursor. The public home page's HTML differs from before in exactly those ten
   lines; noindex still sent. Not tested here: Safari and Firefox themselves (only Chromium is installed);
   the logic no longer depends on anything browser-specific, and the native cursor shows whenever the
   custom one does not.

   THE STANDING CHECKLIST (run by me on every build before main moves, and by a critic pass after every batch of
   pictures and before any structural build):
    1. In every grid or row where two products stand side by side: one ground, one light direction, one tile size?
    2. With one tint chosen, do all four products read as that one color side by side?
    3. Is every product whole and clear of the edges in its tile and first picture at 1440, 1024 and 390 px, at rest
       and under the pointer?
    4. Counted on the page itself: does every tint of each product show the same views in the same order?
    5. Does every product page open on the product alone on the house ground, under a title that is the product's
       name, with one sentence that says what the product is?
    6. Does every product have a picture in a home in light that lets the room be read, with something of known size
       in frame, and each lamp its off and on pair from one camera?
    7. Clicked on a desktop and on a phone: does every picture, tile, marker and button open the product and tint it
       shows?
    8. Does every room picture name the products in it as links, and is every marker off the drivers, the lit
       shades, the record and other controls?
    9. Is there no line beside a picture that says what the picture shows, and no new coined or mood line?
   10. Does one visible button on every product page, on the first phone screen or fixed to its foot, open a letter
       that names the product and tint, and does a test letter from an outside account arrive?
   11. Does every product and tint have an address that loads directly, without the opening, and previews with its
       own name and a picture of that product?
   12. Do the page, the line beside the ask and the link preview say the same thing about whether it can be ordered,
       and does each picture's limit (render, "Shown in Ember.") stand beside the picture?
   13. Does a color click keep the view the visitor is on, and does one Back leave the product?
   14. Are sizes and weights given in inches and pounds as well as metric, and do the figures agree with each other,
       with the labels inside the pictures and with the clear tint (Fog)?
   15. Was each changed page set beside the same kind of page at two reference stores (a lamp: DWR and Louis Poulsen;
       the speaker: KEF and Klipsch), with each difference fixed or kept on purpose?

## Tropos
THE VIEW TRANSFORM (10 Nov 00:22): every studio picture is graded through Khronos PBR Neutral, one global curve. Its toe lowers faint
near-black values on saturated colours (a first-surface floor reflection of ~0.035 linear shows as ~0.012 on a red face). The scene stays
physical; the curve is a camera-profile choice, not an honesty issue. AgX or Standard would read the tints lighter and milkier (a look question for the owner at
the corrected re-shoot).
IN-FRAME AUDIT UNPROVEN (9 Nov 23:10): the speaker lane's cast (shots._frame_rays) built the camera differently from render.py
(the sensor fitted to the larger side and the lens aimed, where render.py fits it horizontal and shifts a level camera). On portrait
frames it was 20 % too narrow and pitched wrong: 0cbf23a5's floor cards cast 0 % and showed; two reflect-only back cards stood 4.9 %
in the tall's frame. Fixed in 0c6acd5d. A second error (23:23): with sensor_fit HORIZONTAL Blender measures lens shift in frame
widths, not the larger side; the cast sat 54 rows high on the tall, and the tall's bg-left lamp (camera-hidden) stood in frame,
441 px at the top-left, on every tall rig up to 3603508e. Fixed in fa7665dc (checked against bpy's world_to_camera_view), whose mask
pass now draws every camera-hidden mesh and lit area lamp as well as the equipment. Every "0 hidden in frame" below is being
re-proven by that mask (the speaker lane, one render per distinct camera and set) and by the Mantle lane's gt_audit.py (b730fe6b)
elsewhere; a picture with anything hidden in frame is pulled at once. Verified so far: Mantle by day (41232b56) and the Ice wide
night final (78e440ba), 0 px hidden. Lamps (23:34): the bpy rigs' audit read the camera at its rest pose (fixed; the mask check
hiddencast_bpy.mask_check now rules); clean by the mask: the dome and orb heroes, lamps-domes-7, Core A/B day and night, Hemisphere
night and day, the unlit Ember 4:5 and the full Ember pair; NOT clean: round 3's orb row (see Pulled).
Lights audit (9 Nov 21:40, from each shot.json): the turning views, the horn-backs and the amp plate are lit by key, fill and wash
(area lights) plus background lamps linked to the sweep (flagged background lights); chunks 4 and 05 (06b829ce, 52ad76ba) also carry

stands in frame (16-20 % by the corrected cast; 61,101 px on a horn-back by the mask, 23:27). Every picture carrying it is pulled below. No specular-only source in any of them. The tallr2 fronts carried
three specular-only panels (scrim, sheen, topline at diffuse 0): withdrawn from the re-shoot (21:34) so the fronts are lit only by
